as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: Sometimes, usually when I'm alone at my desk or driving back from an appointment, I hear my name or a short comment like 'fix the schedule' as clearly as if someone is beside me, but when I look there’s no one there.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: I have stretches where I feel unusually upbeat and sure everything is clicking, then a tiny change like someone moving a meeting can make me sharp with people in a way that surprises me.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: During those stretches I start rebuilding work templates, making care plans, emailing relatives, and reorganizing files all at once, like sitting still would mean I’m dropping the ball.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I’ll feel like I’m the only one who can straighten out the doctors, the family, and the office, and I start believing my system is better than anyone else’s judgment.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: There have been nights when I sleep two or three hours, wake up before dawn, and feel ready to answer emails or update appointment lists instead of feeling tired.\n- key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: My wife and coworkers tell me I talk so fast during those periods that I jump from medical forms to work deadlines to mentoring ideas before they can get a word in.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During my high-energy stretches, the certainty that messages or calendar problems meant something special would get stronger, and I would sometimes hear those short comments while I was running on very little sleep.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this whole period, I’ve been cycling back into those driven, overconfident stretches where I sleep less and take on too much, even if some weeks are quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There have been calm weeks when I wasn’t revved up or sleeping less, but for more than a couple of weeks I still heard my name or felt convinced ordinary work comments were coded tests.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I'd say about a year and three months. It really started after a family member of mine... well, their health and behavior started changing pretty significantly, and I had to take on a lot more of the coordination for their care. Since then, it's just been this kind of heavy, flat feeling that hasn't really lifted.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death, or wishing you were dead, during this time?\n\nPatient: No, no... I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I don't wish I were dead or anything. It's just more of a... a lack of energy and enjoyment in things. But no, no thoughts about dying.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has changed. There's no joy in it anymore, which is why I've just stopped trying to initiate any of it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now. I'd say about a year and three months. It really started after a family member of mine... well, their health and behavior started changing pretty significantly, and I had to take on a lot more of the coordination for their care. Since then, it's just been this kind of heavy, flat feeling that hasn't really lifted.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death, or wishing you were dead, during this time?\n\nPatient: No, no... I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I don't wish I were dead or anything. It's just more of a... a lack of energy and enjoyment in things. But no, no thoughts about dying.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has changed. Um... there have been nights lately where I really only sleep for maybe two or three hours. I'll just wake up way before dawn, and instead of feeling exhausted like you'd expect, I don't feel tired at all. I actually feel completely ready to go. Like, I'll wake up and feel ready to just start answering my boss's emails or updating appointment lists for work right then and there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My appetite has been pretty normal, honestly. I haven't noticed myself eating any more or less than usual, so... yeah, no real changes there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't think I'm moving or talking any slower than I usually do. And I wouldn't say I feel restless or fidgety, either. I feel pretty normal in that regard, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I feel sad. I actually feel pretty good, honestly. So, no, sadness hasn't really been an issue for me lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, that's actually what made me come in. That's honestly the main reason I came in to see you. The thought of doing them just feels flat and heavy, so I've completely stopped initiating any of it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I mentioned earlier about not feeling sad, my mood itself is actually fine most of the day. It's really just that flat feeling when it comes to my usual activities, but no... I definitely don't walk around feeling depressed.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any interest in doing things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying a minute ago, no, not really. That's honestly the main reason I came in to see you. The drive to do the things that usually make me feel like myself—going on my photography walks, mentoring the kids, even just keeping a clean calendar—the interest just isn't there right now. The thought of doing them just feels flat and heavy, so I've completely stopped initiating any of it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was just mentioning, that's exactly what made me come in to see you. The things that usually make me feel like myself—the photography walks, mentoring the kids, keeping my calendar in order—they all just started feeling flat and heavy. Since I stopped initiating those, there really hasn't been anything else I look forward to or get any pleasure out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: No, not that I've noticed. My weight has stayed pretty much the same... I haven't had any big drops or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I feel down about not doing my usual stuff, but I don't really sit around feeling guilty or... or like I'm worthless. It's more just that heavy, flat feeling I mentioned, rather than feeling bad about myself as a person.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most things most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Like I was saying before, the things I used to enjoy just don't do anything for me right now. It's pretty much an all-day thing. I just don't have the interest or the energy to care about much of anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood for most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or... or down in the dumps most of the day, if that's what you mean. Like I said, it's more of just a flat feeling where I just don't care about much. But I wouldn't say my actual mood is depressed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, no, not really. I don't feel low or sad. It's really just that flat feeling I mentioned where I just don't have the energy for things. So, no, I wouldn't call it a low mood.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood?\n\nPatient: I feel like we just went over this, but... no. I'm really not in a depressed mood. We keep going in circles on this, but my answer is still the same. It's just that flat feeling I told you about, not depression."
